          <p class="in">(1) have taken a three-credit college course on early childhood development within the past five years;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) have received a baccalaureate or master's degree in early childhood education or school-age child care within the past five years;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) are licensed in Minnesota as a prekindergarten teacher, an early childhood educator, a kindergarten to sixth grade teacher with a prekindergarten specialty, an early childhood special education teacher, or an elementary teacher with a kindergarten endorsement; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) have received a baccalaureate degree with a Montessori certificate within the past five years.</p>
          <p>(c) The director and staff persons, not including substitutes, must complete at least two hours of child development and learning training every second calendar year.</p>
          <p>(d) Substitutes and unsupervised volunteers must complete child development and learning training every second calendar year. There is no minimum number of training hours required.</p>
          <p>(e) Except for training required under paragraph (a), training completed under this subdivision may be used to meet the in-service training requirements under subdivision 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">First aid.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Unless training has been completed within the previous two years, the director, staff persons, substitutes, and unsupervised volunteers must satisfactorily complete pediatric first aid training prior to having unsupervised direct contact with a child, but not to exceed the first 90 days of employment.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Pediatric first aid training must be repeated at least every second calendar year. First aid training under this subdivision must be provided by an individual approved as a first aid instructor and must not be used to meet in-service training requirements under subdivision 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Cardiopulmonary resuscitation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless training has been completed within the previous two years, the director, staff persons, substitutes, and unsupervised volunteers must satisfactorily complete pediatric cardiopulmonary resuscitation (CPR) training that meets the requirements of this subdivision. Pediatric CPR training must be completed prior to having unsupervised direct contact with a child, but not to exceed the first 90 days of employment.</p>
          <p>(b) Pediatric CPR training must be provided by an individual approved to provide pediatric CPR instruction.</p>
          <p>(c) The pediatric CPR training must:</p>
          <p>(1) cover CPR techniques for infants and children and the treatment of obstructed airways;</p>
          <p>(2) include instruction, hands-on practice, and an in-person, observed skills assessment under the direct supervision of a CPR instructor; and</p>
          <p>(3) be developed by the American Heart Association, the American Red Cross, or another organization that uses nationally recognized, evidence-based guidelines for CPR.</p>
          <p>(d) Pediatric CPR training must be repeated at least once every second calendar year.</p>
          <p>(e) Pediatric CPR training in this subdivision must not be used to meet in-service training requirements under subdivision 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Sudden unexpected infant death training.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before caring for infants, the director, staff persons, substitutes, unsupervised volunteers, and any other volunteers must receive training on the standards under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46 </a>and on reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death during orientation and each calendar year thereafter.</p>
          <p>(b) Sudden unexpected infant death reduction training required under this subdivision must be at least one-half hour in length. At a minimum, the training must address the risk factors related to sudden unexpected infant death, means of reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death in child care, and license holder communication with parents regarding reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death.</p>
          <p>(c) Except if completed during orientation, training taken under this subdivision may be used to meet the in-service training requirements under subdivision 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Abusive head trauma training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Before caring for children under school age, the director, staff persons, substitutes, and unsupervised volunteers must receive training on the risk of abusive head trauma during orientation and each calendar year thereafter.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Abusive head trauma training under this subdivision must be at least one-half hour in length. At a minimum, the training must address the risk factors related to shaking infants and young children, means to reduce the risk of abusive head trauma in child care, and license holder communication with parents regarding reducing the risk of abusive head trauma.</p>
          <p>(c) Except if completed during orientation, training taken under this subdivision may be used to meet the in-service training requirements under subdivision 9.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall make available for viewing a video presentation on the dangers associated with shaking infants and young children, which may be used in conjunction with the annual training required under paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Child passenger restraint systems; training requirement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> (a) Before a license holder transports a child or children under age eight in a motor vehicle, the person placing the child or children in a passenger restraint must satisfactorily complete training on the proper use and installation of child restraint systems in motor vehicles.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Training required under this subdivision must be repeated at least once every five years. At a minimum, the training must address the proper use of child restraint systems based on the child's size, weight, and age, and the proper installation of a car seat or booster seat in the motor vehicle used by the license holder to transport the child or children.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Training required under this subdivision must be provided by individuals who are certified and approved by the Department of Public Safety, Office of Traffic Safety. License holders may obtain a list of certified and approved trainers through the Department of Public Safety website or by contacting the agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Child care providers that only transport school-age children as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.01#stat.142B.01.25">142B.01, subdivision 25</a>, in child care buses as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.448#stat.169.448.1">169.448, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (e), are exempt from this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(e) Training completed under this subdivision may be used to meet in-service training requirements under subdivision 9. Training completed within the previous five years is transferable upon a staff person's change in employment to another child care center.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">In-service training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A license holder must ensure that the center director, staff persons, substitutes, and unsupervised volunteers complete in-service training each calendar year.</p>
          <p>(b) The center director and staff persons who work more than 20 hours per week must complete 24 hours of in-service training each calendar year. Staff persons who work 20 hours or less per week must complete 12 hours of in-service training each calendar year. Substitutes and unsupervised volunteers must complete the requirements of paragraphs (d) to (g) and do not otherwise have a minimum number of hours of training to complete.</p>
          <p>(c) The number of in-service training hours may be prorated for individuals not employed for an entire year.</p>
          <p>(d) Each year, in-service training must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the center's procedures for maintaining health and safety according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.66">142B.66 </a>and Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0140">9503.0140</a>, and handling emergencies and accidents according to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0110">9503.0110</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) the reporting responsibilities under chapter 260E and Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0130">9503.0130</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) at least one-half hour of training on the standards under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46</a> and on reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death as required under subdivision 6, if applicable; and</p>
          <p>(4) at least one-half hour of training on the risk of abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children as required under subdivision 7, if applicable.</p>
          <p>(e) Each year, or when a change is made, whichever is more frequent, in-service training must be provided on: (1) the center's risk reduction plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.54#stat.142B.54.2">142B.54, subdivision 2</a>; and (2) a child's individual child care program plan as required under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0065">9503.0065</a>, subpart 3.</p>
          <p>(f) At least once every two calendar years, the in-service training must include:</p>
          <p>(1) child development and learning training under subdivision 3;</p>
          <p>(2) pediatric first aid that meets the requirements of subdivision 4;</p>
          <p>(3) pediatric cardiopulmonary resuscitation training that meets the requirements of subdivision 5;</p>
          <p>(4) cultural dynamics training to increase awareness of cultural differences; and</p>
          <p>(5) disabilities training to increase awareness of differing abilities of children.</p>
          <p>(g) At least once every five years, in-service training must include child passenger restraint training that meets the requirements of subdivision 8, if applicable.</p>
          <p>(h) The remaining hours of the in-service training requirement must be met by completing training in the following content areas of the Minnesota Knowledge and Competency Framework:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) Content area I: child development and learning;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) Content area II: developmentally appropriate learning experiences;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) Content area III: relationships with families;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) Content area IV: assessment, evaluation, and individualization;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) Content area V: historical and contemporary development of early childhood education;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) Content area VI: professionalism;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) Content area VII: health, safety, and nutrition; and</p>
          <p class="in">(8) Content area VIII: application through clinical experiences.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) For purposes of this subdivision, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p class="in">(1) "Child development and learning training" means training in understanding how children develop physically, cognitively, emotionally, and socially and learn as part of the children's family, culture, and community.</p>
          <p class="in">(2) "Developmentally appropriate learning experiences" means creating positive learning experiences, promoting cognitive development, promoting social and emotional development, promoting physical development, and promoting creative development.</p>
          <p class="in">(3) "Relationships with families" means training on building a positive, respectful relationship with the child's family.</p>
          <p class="in">(4) "Assessment, evaluation, and individualization" means training in observing, recording, and assessing development; assessing and using information to plan; and assessing and using information to enhance and maintain program quality.</p>
          <p class="in">(5) "Historical and contemporary development of early childhood education" means training in past and current practices in early childhood education and how current events and issues affect children, families, and programs.</p>
          <p class="in">(6) "Professionalism" means training in knowledge, skills, and abilities that promote ongoing professional development.</p>
          <p class="in">(7) "Health, safety, and nutrition" means training in establishing health practices, ensuring safety, and providing healthy nutrition.</p>
          <p>(8) "Application through clinical experiences" means clinical experiences in which a person applies effective teaching practices using a range of educational programming models.</p>
          <p class="in">(j) The license holder must ensure that documentation, as required in subdivision 10, includes the number of total training hours required to be completed, name of the training, the Minnesota Knowledge and Competency Framework content area, number of hours completed, and the director's approval of the training.</p>
          <p>(k) In-service training completed by a staff person that is not specific to that child care center is transferable upon a staff person's change in employment to another child care program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.65.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>All training must be documented and maintained on site in each personnel record. In addition to any requirements for each training provided in this section, documentation for each staff person must include the staff person's first date of direct contact and first date of unsupervised contact with a child in care.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.65.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.21.0">2007 c 112 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/26/#laws.0.3.0">2009 c 26 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/23/#laws.0.2.0">2011 c 23 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.20.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.29.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 29</a>-31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.16.7.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 16 s 7</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.1.10.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 1 s 10</a>; art 8 s 49; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.66">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.66 CHILD CARE CENTER HEALTH AND SAFETY REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.66.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Allergy prevention and response.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before admitting a child for care, the license holder must obtain documentation of any known allergy from the child's parent or legal guardian or the child's source of medical care. If a child has a known allergy, the license holder must maintain current information about the allergy in the child's record and develop an individual child care program plan as specified in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0065">9503.0065</a>, subpart 3. The individual child care program plan must include but not be limited to a description of the allergy, specific triggers, avoidance techniques, symptoms of an allergic reaction, and procedures for responding to an allergic reaction, including medication, dosages, and a doctor's contact information.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must ensure that each staff person who is responsible for carrying out the individual child care program plan review and follow the plan. Documentation of a staff person's review must be kept on site.</p>
          <p>(c) At least once each calendar year or following any changes made to allergy-related information in the child's record, the license holder must update the child's individual child care program plan and inform each staff person who is responsible for carrying out the individual child care program plan of the change. The license holder must keep on site documentation that a staff person was informed of a change.</p>
          <p>(d) A child's allergy information must be available at all times including on site, when on field trips, or during transportation. A child's food allergy information must be readily available to a staff person in the area where food is prepared and served to the child.</p>
          <p>(e) The license holder must contact the child's parent or legal guardian as soon as possible in any instance of exposure or allergic reaction that requires medication or medical intervention. The license holder must call emergency medical services when epinephrine is administered to a child in the license holder's care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.66.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Handling and disposal of bodily fluids.</span></h2>
          <p>The licensed child care center must comply with the following procedures for safely handling and disposing of bodily fluids:</p>
          <p>(1) surfaces that come in contact with potentially infectious bodily fluids, including blood and vomit, must be cleaned and disinfected according to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0005">9503.0005</a>, subpart 11;</p>
          <p>(2) blood-contaminated material must be disposed of in a plastic bag with a secure tie;</p>
          <p>(3) sharp items used for a child with special care needs must be disposed of in a "sharps container." The sharps container must be stored out of reach of a child;</p>
          <p>(4) the license holder must have the following bodily fluid disposal supplies in the center: disposable gloves, disposal bags, and eye protection; and</p>
          <p>(5) the license holder must ensure that each staff person follows universal precautions to reduce the risk of spreading infectious disease.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.66.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Emergency preparedness.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A licensed child care center must have a written emergency plan for emergencies that require evacuation, sheltering, or other protection of a child, such as fire, natural disaster, intruder, or other threatening situation that may pose a health or safety hazard to a child. The plan must be written on a form developed by the commissioner and must include:</p>
          <p>(1) procedures for an evacuation, relocation, shelter-in-place, or lockdown;</p>
          <p>(2) a designated relocation site and evacuation route;</p>
          <p>(3) procedures for notifying a child's parent or legal guardian of the evacuation, relocation, shelter-in-place, or lockdown, including procedures for reunification with families;</p>
          <p>(4) accommodations for a child with a disability or a chronic medical condition;</p>
          <p>(5) procedures for storing a child's medically necessary medicine that facilitates easy removal during an evacuation or relocation;</p>
          <p>(6) procedures for continuing operations in the period during and after a crisis;</p>
          <p>(7) procedures for communicating with local emergency management officials, law enforcement officials, or other appropriate state or local authorities; and</p>
          <p>(8) accommodations for infants and toddlers.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must train staff persons on the emergency plan at orientation, when changes are made to the plan, and at least once each calendar year. Training must be documented in each staff person's personnel file.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder must conduct drills according to the requirements in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0110">9503.0110</a>, subpart 3. The date and time of the drills must be documented.</p>
          <p>(d) The license holder must review and update the emergency plan annually. Documentation of the annual emergency plan review shall be maintained in the program's administrative records.</p>
          <p>(e) The license holder must include the emergency plan in the program's policies and procedures as specified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.10#stat.142B.10.21">142B.10, subdivision 21</a>. The license holder must provide a physical or electronic copy of the emergency plan to the child's parent or legal guardian upon enrollment.</p>
          <p>(f) The relocation site and evacuation route must be posted in a visible place as part of the written procedures for emergencies and accidents in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9503.0140">9503.0140</a>, subpart 21.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.66.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Child passenger restraint requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A license holder must comply with all seat belt and child passenger restraint system requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.685">169.685</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.66.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Telephone requirement in licensed child care centers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A working telephone which is capable of making outgoing calls and receiving incoming calls must be located within the licensed child care center at all times. Staff must have access to a working telephone while providing care and supervision to children in care, even if the care occurs outside of the child care facility. A license holder may use a cellular telephone to meet the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) If a cellular telephone is used to satisfy the requirements of this subdivision, the cellular telephone must be accessible to staff, be stored in a centrally located area when not in use, and be sufficiently charged for use at all times.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.52.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.67">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.67 CHILD CARE CENTER HIRING PRACTICES.</h1>
        <p>As part of the employment assessment process, a child care center license holder or staff person may observe how a prospective employee interacts with children in the licensed facility. The prospective employee is not considered a child care background study subject under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.02#stat.245C.02.6a">245C.02, subdivision 6a</a>, provided the prospective employee is under continuous direct supervision by a staff person when the prospective employee has physical access to a child served by the center. The observation period shall not be longer than two hours, and a prospective employee must not be counted in staff-to-child ratios.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.67.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.26.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.70">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.70 FAMILY CHILD CARE TRAINING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Initial training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) License holders, second adult caregivers, and substitutes must comply with the training requirements in this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Helpers who assist with care on a regular basis must complete six hours of training within one year after the date of initial employment.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Training requirements established under this section that must be completed prior to initial licensure must be satisfied only by a newly licensed child care provider or by a child care provider who has not held an active child care license in Minnesota in the previous 12 months. A child care provider who voluntarily cancels a license or allows the license to lapse for a period of less than 12 months and who seeks reinstatement of the lapsed or canceled license within 12 months of the lapse or cancellation must satisfy the annual, ongoing training requirements, and is not required to satisfy the training requirements that must be completed prior to initial licensure. A child care provider who relocates within the state must (1) satisfy the annual, ongoing training requirements according to the schedules established in this section and (2) not be required to satisfy the training requirements under this section that the child care provider completed prior to initial licensure. If a licensed provider moves to a new county, the new county is prohibited from requiring the provider to complete any orientation class or training for new providers.</p>
          <p>(d) Before a second adult caregiver or substitute cares for a child or assists in the care of a child, the license holder must train the second adult caregiver or substitute on:</p>
          <p>(1) the emergency preparedness plan required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.71#stat.142B.71.3">142B.71, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) allergy prevention and response required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.71#stat.142B.71.1">142B.71, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions and general provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "second adult caregiver" means an adult who cares for children in the licensed program along with the license holder for a cumulative total of more than 500 hours annually;</p>
          <p>(2) "helper" means a minor, ages 13 to 17, who assists in caring for children; and</p>
          <p>(3) "substitute" means an adult who assumes responsibility for a license holder for a cumulative total of not more than 500 hours annually.</p>
          <p class="bl">An adult who cares for children in the licensed program along with the license holder for a cumulative total of not more than 500 hours annually has the same training requirements as a substitute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Child development and learning and behavior guidance training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of family and group family child care, the license holder and each second adult caregiver shall complete and document at least four hours of child development and learning and behavior guidance training prior to initial licensure, and before caring for children. For purposes of this subdivision, "child development and learning training" means training in understanding how children develop physically, cognitively, emotionally, and socially and learn as part of the children's family, culture, and community. "Behavior guidance training" means training in the understanding of the functions of child behavior and strategies for managing challenging situations. The training shall be developed or approved by the commissioner of children, youth, and families.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Notwithstanding initial child development and learning and behavior guidance training requirements in paragraph (a), individuals are exempt from this requirement if they:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) have taken a three-credit course on early childhood development within the past five years;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) have received a baccalaureate or master's degree in early childhood education or school-age child care within the past five years;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) are licensed in Minnesota as a prekindergarten teacher, an early childhood educator, a kindergarten to grade 6 teacher with a prekindergarten specialty, an early childhood special education teacher, or an elementary teacher with a kindergarten endorsement; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) have received a baccalaureate degree with a Montessori certificate within the past five years.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder and each second adult caregiver must annually take at least two hours of child development and learning or behavior guidance training. A three-credit course about early childhood development meets the requirements of this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">First aid.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Before initial licensure and before caring for a child, license holders, second adult caregivers, and substitutes must be trained in pediatric first aid. The first aid training must have been provided by an individual approved to provide first aid instruction. First aid training may be less than eight hours and persons qualified to provide first aid training include individuals approved as first aid instructors. License holders, second adult caregivers, and substitutes must repeat pediatric first aid training every two years within 90 days of the date the training was previously taken.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Video training reviewed and approved by the county licensing agency satisfies the training requirement of this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Cardiopulmonary resuscitation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Before initial licensure and before caring for a child, license holders, second adult caregivers, and substitutes must be trained in pediatric cardiopulmonary resuscitation (CPR), including CPR techniques for infants and children, and in the treatment of obstructed airways. The CPR training must have been provided by an individual approved to provide CPR instruction. License holders, second adult caregivers, and substitutes must repeat pediatric CPR training at least once every two years within 90 days of the date the training was previously taken, and the training must be documented in the license holder's records.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Persons providing CPR training must use CPR training that has been developed:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) by the American Heart Association or the American Red Cross and incorporates psychomotor skills to support the instruction; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) using nationally recognized, evidence-based guidelines for CPR training and incorporates psychomotor skills to support the instruction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) License holders must ensure and document that before the license holder, second adult caregivers, substitutes, and helpers assist in the care of infants, they are instructed on the standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46</a> and receive training on reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death. In addition, license holders must ensure and document that before the license holder, second adult caregivers, substitutes, and helpers assist in the care of infants and children under school age, they receive training on reducing the risk of abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children. The training in this subdivision may be provided as initial training under subdivision 1 or ongoing annual training under subdivision 8.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Sudden unexpected infant death reduction training required under this subdivision must, at a minimum, address the risk factors related to sudden unexpected infant death, means of reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death in child care, and license holder communication with parents regarding reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Abusive head trauma training required under this subdivision must, at a minimum, address the risk factors related to shaking infants and young children, means of reducing the risk of abusive head trauma in child care, and license holder communication with parents regarding reducing the risk of abusive head trauma.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Training for family and group family child care providers must be developed by the commissioner in conjunction with the Minnesota Sudden Infant Death Center and approved by the Minnesota Center for Professional Development. Sudden unexpected infant death reduction training and abusive head trauma training may be provided in a single course of no more than two hours in length.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Sudden unexpected infant death reduction training and abusive head trauma training required under this subdivision must be completed in person or as allowed under subdivision 11, clause (1) or (2), at least once every two years. On the years when the individual receiving training is not receiving training in person or as allowed under subdivision 11, clause (1) or (2), the individual receiving training in accordance with this subdivision must receive sudden unexpected infant death reduction training and abusive head trauma training through a video of no more than one hour in length. The video must be developed or approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(f) An individual who is related to the license holder as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.01#stat.142B.01.15">142B.01, subdivision 15</a>, and who is involved only in the care of the license holder's own infant or child under school age and who is not designated to be a second adult caregiver, helper, or substitute for the licensed program, is exempt from the sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma training.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Child passenger restraint systems; training requirement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A license holder must comply with all seat belt and child passenger restraint system requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.685">169.685</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Family and group family child care programs licensed by the Department of Children, Youth, and Families that serve a child or children under eight years of age must document training that fulfills the requirements in this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(1) Before a license holder, second adult caregiver, substitute, or helper transports a child or children under age eight in a motor vehicle, the person placing the child or children in a passenger restraint must satisfactorily complete training on the proper use and installation of child restraint systems in motor vehicles. Training completed under this subdivision may be used to meet initial training under subdivision 1 or ongoing training under subdivision 8.</p>
          <p class="in">(2) Training required under this subdivision must be at least one hour in length, completed at initial training, and repeated at least once every five years. At a minimum, the training must address the proper use of child restraint systems based on the child's size, weight, and age, and the proper installation of a car seat or booster seat in the motor vehicle used by the license holder to transport the child or children.</p>
          <p class="in">(3) Training under this subdivision must be provided by individuals who are certified and approved by the Department of Public Safety, Office of Traffic Safety. License holders may obtain a list of certified and approved trainers through the Department of Public Safety website or by contacting the agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Child care providers that only transport school-age children as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.01#stat.142B.01.13">142B.01, subdivision 13</a>, paragraph (f), in child care buses as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.448">169.448</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (e), are exempt from this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Training requirements for family and group family child care.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of family and group family child care, the license holder and each second adult caregiver must complete 16 hours of ongoing training each year. Repeat of topical training requirements in subdivisions 3 to 9 shall count toward the annual 16-hour training requirement. Additional ongoing training subjects to meet the annual 16-hour training requirement must be selected from the following areas:</p>
          <p>(1) child development and learning training in understanding how a child develops physically, cognitively, emotionally, and socially, and how a child learns as part of the child's family, culture, and community;</p>
          <p>(2) developmentally appropriate learning experiences, including training in creating positive learning experiences, promoting cognitive development, promoting social and emotional development, promoting physical development, promoting creative development; and behavior guidance;</p>
          <p>(3) relationships with families, including training in building a positive, respectful relationship with the child's family;</p>
          <p>(4) assessment, evaluation, and individualization, including training in observing, recording, and assessing development; assessing and using information to plan; and assessing and using information to enhance and maintain program quality;</p>
          <p>(5) historical and contemporary development of early childhood education, including training in past and current practices in early childhood education and how current events and issues affect children, families, and programs;</p>
          <p>(6) professionalism, including training in knowledge, skills, and abilities that promote ongoing professional development; and</p>
          <p>(7) health, safety, and nutrition, including training in establishing healthy practices; ensuring safety; and providing healthy nutrition.</p>
          <p>(b) A provider who is approved as a trainer through the Develop data system may count up to two hours of training instruction toward the annual 16-hour training requirement in paragraph (a). The provider may only count training instruction hours for the first instance in which they deliver a particular content-specific training during each licensing year. Hours counted as training instruction must be approved through the Develop data system with attendance verified on the trainer's individual learning record and must be in Knowledge and Competency Framework content area VII A (Establishing Healthy Practices) or B (Ensuring Safety).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Other required training requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The training required of family and group family child care providers and staff must include training in the cultural dynamics of early childhood development and child care. The cultural dynamics and disabilities training and skills development of child care providers must be designed to achieve outcomes for providers of child care that include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) an understanding and support of the importance of culture and differences in ability in children's identity development;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) understanding the importance of awareness of cultural differences and similarities in working with children and their families;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) understanding and support of the needs of families and children with differences in ability;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) developing skills to help children develop unbiased attitudes about cultural differences and differences in ability;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) developing skills in culturally appropriate caregiving; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) developing skills in appropriate caregiving for children of different abilities.</p>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall approve the curriculum for cultural dynamics and disability training.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The provider must meet the training requirement in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.41#stat.142B.41.9">142B.41, subdivision 9</a>, paragraph (a), clause (4), to be eligible to allow a child cared for at the family child care or group family child care home to use the swimming pool located at the home.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Supervising for safety; training requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Courses required by this subdivision must include the following health and safety topics:</p>
          <p>(1) preventing and controlling infectious diseases;</p>
          <p>(2) administering medication;</p>
          <p>(3) preventing and responding to allergies;</p>
          <p>(4) ensuring building and physical premises safety;</p>
          <p>(5) handling and storing biological contaminants;</p>
          <p>(6) preventing and reporting child abuse and maltreatment; and</p>
          <p>(7) emergency preparedness.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Before initial licensure and before caring for a child, all family child care license holders and each second adult caregiver shall complete and document the completion of the six-hour Supervising for Safety for Family Child Care course developed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder must ensure and document that, before caring for a child, all substitutes have completed the four-hour Basics of Licensed Family Child Care for Substitutes course developed by the commissioner, which must include health and safety topics as well as child development and learning.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The family child care license holder and each second adult caregiver shall complete and document:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the annual completion of either:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) a two-hour active supervision course developed by the commissioner; or</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) any courses in the ensuring safety competency area under the health, safety, and nutrition standard of the Knowledge and Competency Framework that the commissioner has identified as an active supervision training course; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the completion at least once every five years of the two-hour courses Health and Safety I and Health and Safety II. A license holder's or second adult caregiver's completion of either training in a given year meets the annual active supervision training requirement in clause (1).</p>
          <p>(e) At least once every three years, license holders must ensure and document that substitutes have completed the four-hour Basics of Licensed Family Child Care for Substitutes course.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Approved training.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of children, youth, and families must post information on the department's website indicating the specific category within the Knowledge and Competency Framework that will satisfy training requirements for child development and learning, behavior guidance, and active supervision. County licensing staff must accept trainings designated as satisfying training requirements by the commissioner under this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(b) Unless specifically authorized in this section, one training does not fulfill two different training requirements. Courses within the identified knowledge and competency areas that are specific to child care centers or legal nonlicensed providers do not fulfill the requirements of this section.</p>
          <p>(c) County licensing staff must accept training approved by the Minnesota Center for Professional Development, including:</p>
          <p>(1) face-to-face or classroom training;</p>
          <p>(2) online training; and</p>
          <p>(3) relationship-based professional development, such as mentoring, coaching, and consulting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.70.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Provider training.</span></h2>
          <p>New and increased training requirements under this section must not be imposed on providers until the commissioner establishes statewide accessibility to the required provider training.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.22.0">2007 c 112 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/26/#laws.0.4.0">2009 c 26 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/142/#laws.2.21.0">2009 c 142 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/329/#laws.1.11.0">2010 c 329 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.21.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/228/#laws.2.14.0">2014 c 228 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.1.4.0">2014 c 291 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.47.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.32.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.7.24.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 7 s 24</a>; art 16 s 14-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.53.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.1.11.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 2 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.27.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 27</a>-31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.15.3.0">2024 c 115 art 15 s 3</a>,4; art 16 s 41</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.71">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.71 FAMILY CHILD CARE HEALTH AND SAFETY REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.71.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Allergy prevention and response.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before admitting a child for care, the license holder must obtain information about any known allergy from the child's parent or legal guardian. The license holder must maintain current allergy information in each child's record. The allergy information must include a description of the allergy, specific triggers, avoidance techniques, symptoms of an allergic reaction, and procedures for responding to an allergic reaction, including medication, dosages, and a doctor's contact information.</p>
          <p>(b) The child's allergy information must be documented on a form approved by the commissioner, readily available to all caregivers, and reviewed annually by the license holder and each caregiver.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.71.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Handling and disposal of bodily fluids.</span></h2>
          <p>The licensed family child care provider must comply with the following procedures for safely handling and disposing of bodily fluids:</p>
          <p>(1) surfaces that come in contact with potentially infectious bodily fluids, including blood and vomit, must be cleaned and disinfected as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.76">142B.76</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) blood-contaminated material must be disposed of in a plastic bag with a secure tie;</p>
          <p>(3) sharp items used for a child with special care needs must be disposed of in a "sharps container." The sharps container must be stored out of reach of a child; and</p>
          <p>(4) the license holder must have the following bodily fluid disposal supplies available: disposable gloves, disposal bags, and eye protection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.71.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Emergency preparedness plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A licensed family child care provider must have a written emergency preparedness plan for emergencies that require evacuation, sheltering, or other protection of children, such as fire, natural disaster, intruder, or other threatening situation that may pose a health or safety hazard to children. The plan must be written on a form developed by the commissioner and updated at least annually. The plan must include:</p>
          <p>(1) procedures for an evacuation, relocation, shelter-in-place, or lockdown;</p>
          <p>(2) a designated relocation site and evacuation route;</p>
          <p>(3) procedures for notifying a child's parent or legal guardian of the evacuation, shelter-in-place, or lockdown, including procedures for reunification with families;</p>
          <p>(4) accommodations for a child with a disability or a chronic medical condition;</p>
          <p>(5) procedures for storing a child's medically necessary medicine that facilitate easy removal during an evacuation or relocation;</p>
          <p>(6) procedures for continuing operations in the period during and after a crisis;</p>
          <p>(7) procedures for communicating with local emergency management officials, law enforcement officials, or other appropriate state or local authorities; and</p>
          <p>(8) accommodations for infants and toddlers.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must train caregivers before the caregiver provides care and at least annually on the emergency preparedness plan and document completion of this training.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder must conduct drills according to the requirements in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0435">9502.0435</a>, subpart 8. The date and time of the drills must be documented.</p>
          <p>(d) The license holder must have the emergency preparedness plan available for review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.71.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Transporting children.</span></h2>
          <p>A license holder must ensure compliance with all seat belt and child passenger restraint system requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.685">169.685</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.71.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Telephone requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0435">9502.0435</a>, subpart 8, item B, a license holder is not required to post a list of emergency numbers. A license holder may use a cellular telephone to meet the requirements of Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0435">9502.0435</a>, subpart 8, if the cellular telephone is sufficiently charged for use at all times.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.16.17.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 16 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.54.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 54</a>-56; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.72">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.72 FAMILY CHILD CARE PHYSICAL SPACE REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Means of escape.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) At least one emergency escape route separate from the main exit from the space must be available in: (1) each room used for sleeping by anyone receiving licensed care; and (2) a basement used for child care. One means of escape must be a stairway or door leading to the floor of exit discharge. The other must be a door or window leading directly outside. A window used as an emergency escape route must be openable without special knowledge.</p>
          <p>(b) In homes with construction that began before March 31, 2020, the interior of the window leading directly outside must have a net clear opening area of not less than 4.5 square feet or 648 square inches and have minimum clear opening dimensions of 20 inches wide and 20 inches high. The net clear opening dimensions shall be the result of normal operation of the opening. The opening must be no higher than 48 inches from the floor. The height to the window may be measured from a platform if a platform is located below the window.</p>
          <p>(c) In homes with construction that began on or after March 31, 2020, the interior of the window leading directly outside must have minimum clear opening dimensions of 20 inches wide and 24 inches high. The net clear opening dimensions shall be the result of normal operation of the opening. The opening must be no higher than 44 inches from the floor.</p>
          <p class="bl"> Additional requirements are dependent on the distance of the openings from the ground outside the window: (1) windows or other openings with a sill height not more than 44 inches above or below the finished ground level adjacent to the opening (grade-floor emergency escape and rescue openings) must have a minimum opening of five square feet; and (2) non-grade-floor emergency escape and rescue openings must have a minimum opening of 5.7 square feet.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Door to attached garage.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If there is an opening between an attached garage and a day care residence, there must be a door that is:</p>
          <p>(1) a solid wood bonded-core door at least 1-3/8 inches thick;</p>
          <p>(2) a steel insulated door at least 1-3/8 inches thick; or</p>
          <p>(3) a door with a fire protection rating of 20 minutes.</p>
          <p>(b) The separation wall on the garage side between the residence and garage must consist of 1/2-inch-thick gypsum wallboard or its equivalent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Heating and venting systems.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0425">9502.0425</a>, subpart 7, item C, items that can be ignited and support combustion, including but not limited to plastic, fabric, and wood products must not be located within:</p>
          <p>(1) 18 inches of a gas or fuel-oil heater or furnace; or</p>
          <p>(2) 36 inches of a solid-fuel-burning appliance.</p>
          <p>(b) If a license holder produces manufacturer instructions listing a smaller distance, then the manufacturer instructions control the distance combustible items must be from gas, fuel-oil, or solid-fuel burning heaters or furnaces.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Fire extinguisher.</span></h2>
          <p>A portable, operational, multipurpose, dry chemical fire extinguisher with a minimum 2 A 10 BC rating must be located in or near the kitchen and cooking areas of the residence at all times. The fire extinguisher must be serviced annually by a qualified inspector. All caregivers must know how to properly use the fire extinguisher.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Carbon monoxide and smoke alarms.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All homes must have an approved and operational carbon monoxide alarm installed within ten feet of each room used for sleeping children in care.</p>
          <p>(b) Smoke alarms that have been listed by the Underwriter Laboratory must be properly installed and maintained in hallways outside of rooms used for sleeping children and on all levels, including basements but not including crawl spaces and uninhabitable attics.</p>
          <p>(c) In homes with construction that began on or after March 31, 2020, smoke alarms must be installed and maintained in each room used for sleeping children in care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Updates.</span></h2>
          <p>After readoption of the Minnesota State Fire Code, the fire marshal must notify the commissioner of any changes that conflict with this section and Minnesota Rules, chapter 9502. The state fire marshal must identify necessary statutory changes to align statutes with the revised code. The commissioner must recommend updates to sections of this chapter that are derived from the Minnesota State Fire Code in the legislative session following readoption of the code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Fire code variances.</span></h2>
          <p>When a variance is requested of the standards contained in subdivision 1, 2, 3, 4, or 5, an applicant or provider must submit written approval from the state fire marshal of the variance requested and the alternative measures identified to ensure the safety of children in care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.72.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Stairways.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All stairways must meet the requirements in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) Stairways of four or more steps must have handrails on at least one side.</p>
          <p>(c) Any open area between the handrail and stair tread must be enclosed with a protective guardrail as specified in the State Building Code. At open risers, openings located more than 30 inches or 762 millimeters as measured vertically to the floor or grade below must not permit the passage of a sphere four inches or 102 millimeters in diameter.</p>
          <p>(d) Gates or barriers must be used when children aged six to 18 months are in care.</p>
          <p>(e) Stairways must be well lit, in good repair, and free of clutter and obstructions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.72.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.57.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.125.0">2023 c 25 s 125</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.32.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 32</a>-35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a>; art 19 s 7,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.74">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.74 SUBSTITUTE CAREGIVERS AND REPLACEMENTS IN FAMILY CHILD CARE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.74.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Total hours allowed.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0365">9502.0365</a>, subpart 5, the use of a substitute caregiver in a licensed family child care program must be limited to a cumulative total of not more than 500 hours annually. The license holder must document the name, dates, and number of hours of the substitute who provided care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.74.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Emergency replacement supervision.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A license holder may allow an adult who has not completed the training requirements under this chapter or the background study requirements under chapter 245C to supervise children in a family child care program in an emergency. For purposes of this subdivision, an emergency is a situation in which:</p>
          <p>(1) the license holder has begun operating the family child care program for the day and for reasons beyond the license holder's control, including, but not limited to a serious illness or injury, accident, or situation requiring the license holder's immediate attention, the license holder needs to leave the licensed space and close the program for the day; and</p>
          <p>(2) the parents or guardians of the children attending the program are contacted to pick up their children as soon as is practicable.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must make reasonable efforts to minimize the time the emergency replacement has unsupervised contact with the children in care, not to exceed 24 hours per emergency incident.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder shall not knowingly use a person as an emergency replacement who has committed an action or has been convicted of a crime that would cause the person to be disqualified from providing care to children, if a background study was conducted under chapter 245C.</p>
          <p>(d) To the extent practicable, the license holder must attempt to arrange for emergency care by a substitute caregiver before using an emergency replacement.</p>
          <p>(e) To the extent practicable, the license holder must notify the county licensing agency within seven days that an emergency replacement was used, and specify the circumstances that led to the use of the emergency replacement. The county licensing agency must notify the commissioner within three business days after receiving the license holder's notice that an emergency replacement was used, and specify the circumstances that led to the use of the emergency replacement.</p>
          <p>(f) Notwithstanding the requirements in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0405">9502.0405</a>, a license holder is not required to provide the names of persons who may be used as replacements in emergencies to parents or the county licensing agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.74.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.58.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.75">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.75 FAMILY CHILD CARE INFANT SLEEP SUPERVISION REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.75.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">In-person checks on infants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) License holders of family child care programs that serve infants are encouraged to monitor sleeping infants by conducting in-person checks on each infant in their care every 30 minutes.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon enrollment of an infant in a family child care program, the license holder is encouraged to conduct in-person checks on the sleeping infant every 15 minutes, during the first four months of care.</p>
          <p>(c) When an infant has an upper respiratory infection, the license holder is encouraged to conduct in-person checks on the sleeping infant every 15 minutes throughout the hours of sleep.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142B.75.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of audio or visual monitoring devices.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to conducting the in-person checks encouraged under subdivision 1, license holders serving infants are encouraged to use and maintain an audio or visual monitoring device to monitor each sleeping infant in care during all hours of sleep.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.75.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.18.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.62.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 62</a>,74; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.76">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.76 FAMILY CHILD CARE DIAPERING AREA DISINFECTION.</h1>
        <p class="in">Notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0435">9502.0435</a>, a family child care provider may disinfect the diaper changing surface with chlorine bleach in a manner consistent with label directions for disinfection or with a surface disinfectant that meets the following criteria:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) the manufacturer's label or instructions state that the product is registered with the United States Environmental Protection Agency;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) the manufacturer's label or instructions state that the disinfectant is effective against Staphylococcus aureus, Salmonella enterica, and Pseudomonas aeruginosa;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) the manufacturer's label or instructions state that the disinfectant is effective with a ten minute or less contact time;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) the disinfectant is clearly labeled by the manufacturer with directions for mixing and use;</p>
        <p class="in">(5) the disinfectant is used only in accordance with the manufacturer's directions; and</p>
        <p class="in">(6) the product does not include triclosan or derivatives of triclosan.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.76.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.17.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.18.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.77">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.77 SUPERVISION OF FAMILY CHILD CARE LICENSE HOLDER'S OWN CHILD.</h1>
        <p>(a) Notwithstanding Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9502.0365">9502.0365</a>, subpart 5, and with the license holder's consent, an individual may be present in the licensed space, may supervise the family child care license holder's own child both inside and outside of the licensed space, and is exempt from the training and supervision requirements of this chapter and Minnesota Rules, chapter 9502, if the individual:</p>
        <p>(1) is related to the license holder or to the license holder's child, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.01#stat.142B.01.15">142B.01, subdivision 15</a>, or is a household member who the license holder has reported to the county agency;</p>
        <p>(2) is not a designated caregiver, helper, or substitute for the licensed program;</p>
        <p>(3) is involved only in the care of the license holder's own child; and</p>
        <p>(4) does not have direct, unsupervised contact with any nonrelative children receiving services.</p>
        <p>(b) If the individual in paragraph (a) is not a household member, the individual is also exempt from background study requirements under chapter 245C.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.77.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.46.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.1.8.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.78">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.78 REGULATION OF FAMILY DAY CARE BY LOCAL GOVERNMENT.</h1>
        <p>The authority of local units of government to establish requirements for family day care programs is limited by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299F.011#stat.299F.011.4a">299F.011, subdivision 4a</a>, clauses (1) and (2).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.78.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.16.0">1987 c 333 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.79">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.79 CONTRACTORS SERVING MULTIPLE FAMILY CHILD CARE LICENSE HOLDERS.</h1>
        <p class="in">Contractors who serve multiple family child care license holders may request that the county agency maintain a record of:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) the contractor's background study results as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.04">245C.04</a>, subdivision 8, to verify that the contractor does not have a disqualification or a disqualification that has not been set aside, and is eligible to provide direct contact services in a licensed program; and</p>
        <p class="in">(2) the contractor's compliance with training requirements.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.79.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.1.3.0">2014 c 291 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.41.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142B.80">
        <h1 class="shn">142B.80 CHILD FOSTER CARE TRAINING REQUIREMENT; MENTAL HEALTH TRAINING; FETAL ALCOHOL SPECTRUM DISORDERS TRAINING.</h1>
        <p class="in">Prior to a nonemergency placement of a child in a foster care home, the child foster care license holder and caregivers in foster family and treatment foster care settings must complete two hours of training that addresses the causes, symptoms, and key warning signs of mental health disorders; cultural considerations; and effective approaches for dealing with a child's behaviors. At least one hour of the annual training requirement for the foster family license holder and caregivers must be on children's mental health issues and treatment. Except for providers and services under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D">245D</a>, the annual training must also include at least one hour of training on fetal alcohol spectrum disorders, which must be counted toward the 12 hours of required in-service training per year. Short-term substitute caregivers are exempt from these requirements. Training curriculum shall be approved by the commissioner of children, youth, and families.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142B.80.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.31.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 31; 2024 c 115 art 16 s 40</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
